        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Flexible AI-Generated Animation Sequences

## Overview
Reigh already has a deliberately safe v1 sequence path: `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/` generates trusted structured drafts, `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx` invokes it, `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/` validates and materializes asset-backed params, and `src/tools/video-editor/lib/sequence-drafts.ts` inserts or replaces clips. Rendering is already split by `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts`: native timelines stay on the client path, while themed clips route to `banodoco_render_timeline` / Banodoco workers. Timeline persistence is schema-backed through `timelines.config`, `timelines.asset_registry`, `update_timeline_versioned`, `timeline-import`, and `SupabaseDataProvider`. ArtAgents already emits Reigh-compatible `hype.timeline.json` / `hype.assets.json` and renders with its Remotion wrapper.

The document at `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` should propose an additive architecture: preserve the current trusted sequence creator exactly as the default safe path, then add two higher-flexibility lanes behind explicit capabilities: a declarative schema-sequence lane and a generated Remotion module lane. The architecture must define contracts, validation, sandboxing, storage, migration, ArtAgents parity, testing, deployment, and rollout without implying raw untrusted code should be executed in the current editor/browser path.

## Phase 1: Document Current State And Invariants

### Step 1: Author the current-state map (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Document** the existing safe path in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: edge generation in `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.ts`, prompt/repair in `templates.ts`, duplicate frontend validator in `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/validation.ts`, trusted metadata in `metadata.ts`, draft insertion/replacement in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/sequence-drafts.ts`, and materialization in `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.ts`.
2. **Call out** the invariant that current trusted draft output is data-only: `{ clipType, hold, params }`, no JSX/code/imports/raw URLs/animation refs.
3. **Name** the existing renderer split in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts`: native clips use the existing client renderer; registered themed clips route to Banodoco/Remotion.

### Step 2: Define non-negotiable constraints (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a constraints section to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` stating that the current trusted sequence path remains default, backwards-compatible, and deployable independently.
2. **Specify** that higher-flexibility generation must be capability-gated by lane, not by weakening the current validator.
3. **State** that persisted timelines continue to use `timelines.config` and `timelines.asset_registry`, with versioned saves through `update_timeline_versioned` or `timeline-import`.

## Phase 2: Target Architecture — Three Sequence Lanes

### Step 3: Design lane 1, trusted structured drafts (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Describe** the existing lane as `trusted_sequence_v1` in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`.
2. **Preserve** current clip types (`image-jump`, `section-hook`, `art-card`, `resource-card`, `cta-card`) and validation behavior.
3. **Document** its intended use: fast safe prompt-to-timeline drafting and editing with existing UI controls.

### Step 4: Design lane 2, declarative schema-sequences (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Add** a schema-sequence contract to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` for AI-authored animation data that is richer than v1 but still non-code: version, lane, duration, fps hints, tracks/clips, typed primitives, bindings, keyframes, transitions, asset refs, theme refs, constraints, and provenance.
2. **Anchor** it to existing timeline schema fields from `src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts` and `src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.ts`, extending through explicit `params`, `generation`, and optional metadata fields rather than arbitrary top-level keys.
3. **Define** a compiler step: `schema_sequence` -> canonical `TimelineConfig` + `AssetRegistry`, then existing editor/render paths consume the result.
4. **Specify** validator behavior: JSON schema/Zod validation, finite numeric bounds, max clip/track/keyframe counts, only registry asset keys or signed/public storage keys, deterministic defaults, no code strings, no network URLs unless already present in the asset registry.

### Step 5: Design lane 3, generated Remotion modules (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Add** a generated-code lane to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` that treats AI-authored Remotion modules as isolated render artifacts, not inline editor runtime code.
2. **Define** module shape: manifest JSON, source bundle, entry component, input props schema, asset contract, duration/dimensions/fps contract, declared capabilities, dependency allowlist, and provenance.
3. **State** the execution boundary: generated modules render only in a worker sandbox/Remotion render service, never directly in the Reigh browser editor.
4. **Specify** sandboxing: no arbitrary npm install at render time, no Node filesystem/network except injected asset reads and output writes, static import allowlist, bundle-time lint/typecheck, timeout/memory/frame limits, deterministic props, console/log capture, and artifact quarantine on validation failure.
5. **Connect** to render routing: generated-code clips always route to Banodoco/Remotion render, while editor preview shows a placeholder, last rendered preview, or schema-derived fallback.

## Phase 3: Reigh Data Model, Edge Functions, And Storage

### Step 6: Plan database and storage contracts (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Document** minimal additive tables/columns in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: a `generated_sequence_artifacts` table for manifests, schema payloads, code bundle refs, validation status, author/user/project/timeline ids, source model, hash, and timestamps.
2. **Avoid** replacing `timelines.config`; instead store compiled timeline clips in existing config and link clips to artifacts through `clip.generation` or a narrow artifact reference field if the schema package is extended.
3. **Define** storage buckets/paths: keep media in `timeline-assets`; use a separate private bucket/path for generated module source/bundles/previews if code artifacts are introduced; final renders stay aligned with existing render output conventions.
4. **Preserve** RLS/user ownership semantics matching `timelines` and `timeline-assets`.

### Step 7: Plan edge function changes (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Add** an edge-function section to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` describing an additive `ai-generate-sequence-v2` or versioned mode on `ai-generate-sequence`, with explicit `lane: trusted | schema | remotion_module`.
2. **Keep** `ai-generate-sequence` v1 responses compatible unless the caller opts into v2.
3. **Define** validation pipeline: prompt -> model output -> parse -> lane-specific validator -> repair loop -> artifact persistence -> compiled timeline preview payload.
4. **Document** imports/reuse from existing helpers: rate limit conventions, `bootstrapEdgeHandler`, `jsonResponse`, `toErrorMessage`, and structured invalid draft reporting.
5. **Specify** `timeline-import` integration for external/ArtAgents-written timeline updates, including version mismatch behavior and asset registry validation.

### Step 8: Plan renderer contracts (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Document** renderer input/output contracts in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: canonical `TimelineConfig`, `AssetRegistry`, theme id, theme overrides, output filename, project/user/timeline ids, correlation id, and render artifact URL.
2. **Require** `materializeSequenceConfig`-style asset resolution before worker handoff for sequence params that expose component-facing URLs.
3. **Define** failure modes: validation failure, unsupported capability, module compile failure, render timeout, missing asset, and version conflict should all be structured errors visible to the editor.

## Phase 4: Reigh Editor And Migration Path

### Step 9: Plan editor UX and migration (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** an editor integration section to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` that keeps `SequenceCreatorPanel` on trusted drafts by default.
2. **Define** opt-in controls for schema/code lanes: capability-gated generate mode, validation report, preview status, insert/replace behavior, and safe fallback when a generated module cannot be previewed interactively.
3. **Plan** migration from current trusted sequence creator: ship lane metadata first, then schema-sequence generation, then worker-only generated modules; no existing timeline rows require migration.
4. **State** that v1 trusted drafts can be compiled into the v2 artifact model later for observability, but this should be a passive migration, not a blocking launch task.

### Step 10: Plan ArtAgents parity (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Document** ArtAgents touch points in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: `artagents/timeline.py`, `artagents/render_remotion.py`, `artagents/open_in_reigh.py`, `artagents/publish.py`, `remotion/src/Root.tsx`, generated Remotion types, and `hype.timeline.json` / `hype.assets.json` outputs.
2. **Require** shared schema parity: ArtAgents should be able to emit the same declarative schema-sequence artifacts and compiled Reigh `TimelineConfig`/`AssetRegistry` as Reigh.
3. **Define** generated Remotion module parity: ArtAgents can author/render modules locally through its Remotion wrapper, then publish compatible artifacts to Reigh only after validation.
4. **Call out** the current limitation from `open_in_reigh.py`: local absolute asset paths must be uploaded or self-hosted before Reigh playback.

## Phase 5: Validation, Tests, Deployment, And Rollout

### Step 11: Plan automated validation and tests (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Add** a test matrix to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` with unit tests for validators, schema compilers, serialization, render routing, artifact persistence, edge handlers, and timeline import.
2. **Name** existing Reigh test anchors: `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.test.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/validation.test.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.test.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/sequence-drafts.test.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.test.ts`, `supabase/functions/timeline-import/handler.test.ts`, and timeline-agent harness tests under `supabase/functions/_tests/harness/`.
3. **Name** ArtAgents test anchors: Remotion smoke tests, timeline schema contract tests, render wrapper tests, publish/open-in-Reigh tests, and generated registry/type generation checks.
4. **Require** negative tests for code injection, raw URLs, missing assets, oversized schemas, unsupported imports, timeouts, version conflicts, and cross-user access.

### Step 12: Plan deployment and rollout (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** a rollout section to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: feature flags by lane, internal allowlist, shadow validation, artifact-only dry runs, limited schema insertion, worker-only code render beta, then broader release.
2. **Define** observability: validation error counts, repair loop counts, compile/render failures, render duration, artifact hash, lane usage, and user-visible failure rates.
3. **Include** rollback: disable schema/code lanes independently, leave trusted v1 operational, preserve existing timelines, and hide generated module insert controls without deleting artifacts.

### Step 13: Final document verification (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` contains sections for declarative schema-sequence, generated Remotion modules, edge functions, renderer/data model contracts, validation/sandboxing, storage, ArtAgents parity, migration from trusted sequence creator, tests, deployment, and rollout.
2. **Verify** `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` explicitly states that the current safe trusted sequence path is preserved and remains the default.
3. **Verify** all concrete file references in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` match existing repository paths or are clearly labeled as proposed new files/tables.

## Execution Order
1. Write the current-state and invariants in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` first so the plan is anchored in the existing system.
2. Define the three-lane architecture before database or edge changes; storage and renderer contracts depend on the lane boundaries.
3. Document Reigh runtime contracts before ArtAgents parity so ArtAgents targets the same canonical shapes.
4. Finish with validation, deployment, and rollout because those sections depend on all earlier contracts.

## Validation Order
1. First verify the document path and section coverage in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`.
2. Then check that current safe v1 behavior is explicitly preserved and not weakened.
3. Then check that proposed schema/code flexibility has concrete sandbox, validation, storage, and renderer boundaries.
4. Finally check that tests and rollout include negative/security cases, ArtAgents parity, and rollback controls.

      "plan_ids": [
        "roll-back-the-first-class-20260408-1337"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-132",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 11.2 specifies the reverse switch is 'single commit, no `start`/`children`/`offset` reads anywhere' but doesn't state the mutation type explicitly. since the step describes metadeletes + cliporderoverride + pinnedshotgroupsoverride in a single commit, the mutation must be `type: 'rows'` (the only variant carrying all three). self-consistent with step 11.1's restriction on `type: 'pinnedshotgroups'`, but making the mutation type explicit would reduce executor ambiguity.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "roll-back-the-first-class-20260408-1337"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-135",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 3 against the actual `useselectedmediaclips()` inputs in [useselectedmediaclips.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). that hook currently reads only `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig` from editor context, so the plan's literal instruction to join `resolvedconfig.pinnedshotgroups` with `useshots()` data will not compile unless it first switches to `data?.config.pinnedshotgroups` or another real source of pinned groups.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-first-class-shot-20260409-2052"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-149",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual timeline selection ownership across `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelineselection.ts`, and `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usemultiselect.ts`. `usetimelinecommit` owns only `selectedclipid` and `selectedtrackid`, while `usemultiselect` still owns `selectedclipids`, `primaryclipid`, and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan's step 3 is technically incomplete: following it literally would leave timeline selection split between the new store and old hook-local state instead of collapsing it into one authoritative store.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-150",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the current imperative selection adapters in `useclipdrag.ts`, `usetimelinepersistence.ts`, `usepollsync.ts`, and `videoeditorprovider.tsx`. the mutable refs (`selectedclipidsref`, `additiveselectionref`, `selectedclipidref`, `selectedtrackidref`) are runtime adapters for drag/save/poll callers rather than durable domain state, so the plan's wording about putting 'refs/helpers' into the `timeline` slice is a design error unless it explicitly keeps store state separate from ref adapters rebuilt around that state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-166",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` and `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/tracklistrenderer.tsx`. `timelinecanvas` still computes shared `rowresizepreview`, `selectedtrackid`, and other drag-sensitive props centrally and passes them through every `sortablerow`, so moving context reads to narrow zustand selectors alone will not meet the gate that non-dragged rows stay at 0 rerenders during drag unless the plan also changes this row-prop fan-out.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-167",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the staged-vs-mounted add flow in `src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useaddtovideoeditor.ts`. that hook decides between immediate in-process drop and staged url/localstorage fallback by testing whether the safe timeline hooks return `null`, so a global `timelinestore.ts` with default state would change runtime behavior unless the migration preserves an explicit 'editor mounted' availability boundary.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-168",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.ts` and `src/tools/video-editor/contexts/videoeditorprovider.tsx` together. the current composition is synchronous and consumers like the pending-add drain and agentchat bridge read fresh `editor` values in the same render pass, so the plan's 'build values and write them into the zustand store' step is under-specified about whether hydration happens during render or in an effect, and either choice has real correctness risk if not designed carefully.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the `usepinnedgroupsync()` call site in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479` against the proposed context-based interaction guard. because sync is called directly from `timelineeditor`, routing its guard through new editor-context plumbing is not the most direct fit for the actual caller and depends on extra provider/type changes that the step does not enumerate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "refactor-pinned-shot-groups-20260408-0508"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-138",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the caller contract of `useselectedmediaclips()` via [usetimelineeditordata](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). this hook's caller currently provides `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig`, not a resolved config object with `pinnedshotgroups`, so the plan still does not handle the actual arguments passed at that call site and needs to pull pinned groups from editor `data` instead.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-first-class-shot-20260409-2052"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual callers of the timeline-selection refs with `rg 'selectedclipidsref|additiveselectionref|selectedclipidref|selectedtrackidref' src/tools/video-editor`. save/poll paths consume `selectedclipidref` and `selectedtrackidref`, but `useclipdrag.ts`, `timelineeditor.tsx`, `videoeditorprovider.tsx`, and `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts` also consume `selectedclipidsref` and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan does not yet handle all caller shapes unless it adds a store-backed adapter for the multi-select refs as well.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `setselectedclipid(` callers in `useassetmanagement.ts`, `usecliptextoverlay.ts`, `propertiespanel.tsx`, and `previewpanel.tsx`. those callers pass concrete clip ids and `null` through the existing dispatch-like api, so any store action that replaces `setselectedclipid` must preserve the same semantics for clearing and reselection rather than narrowing the contract to a simpler single-purpose setter.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-175",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the real callers of the safe timeline hooks in `src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useaddtovideoeditor.ts`. that code passes through both mounted-editor values and the `null` outside-provider case, then uses `ops.registergenerationasset`, `ops.handleassetdrop`, and `data.resolvedconfig?.clips` when available, so any replacement api has to preserve both nullable access and the current immediate-drop contract rather than only exposing non-null store selectors.",

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` as a caller of `usetimelineeditordata()`. it only reads `dataref`, but it passes that mutable ref straight into `useclipresizegesture`, which relies on latest timeline data during pointer sequences; the plan does not yet say how this exact caller will receive a stable mutable adapter once the context is removed.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      "id": "DEBT-177",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/videoeditorshell.tsx`, where `handlekeyboarddelete` combines `editordata.dataref.current` with `editordata.selectedclipids` to build a mutation before falling back to `handledeleteclips`. that caller needs synchronized latest-ref and latest-selection semantics, so splitting those reads across unrelated store selectors without an adapter can change behavior even if each field is individually correct.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]

        Debt guidance:
        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.
        - When a concern is recurring debt that still needs to be flagged, prefix it with `Recurring debt:` after the subsystem tag, for example `Timeout recovery: Recurring debt: retry backoff remains brittle`.



        Your output template is at: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/plans/build-a-very-robust-plan-for-20260501-2249/critique_output.json
Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Flexible AI-Generated Animation Sequences

## Overview
Reigh already has a deliberately safe v1 sequence path: `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/` generates trusted structured drafts, `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx` invokes it, `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/` validates and materializes asset-backed params, and `src/tools/video-editor/lib/sequence-drafts.ts` inserts or replaces clips. Rendering is already split by `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts`: native timelines stay on the client path, while registered themed clips route to `banodoco_render_timeline` / Banodoco workers. Timeline persistence is schema-backed through `timelines.config`, `timelines.asset_registry`, `update_timeline_versioned`, `timeline-import`, and `SupabaseDataProvider`. ArtAgents already emits Reigh-compatible `hype.timeline.json` / `hype.assets.json` and renders with its Remotion wrapper.

The critique does not show the plan targeting the wrong root cause. The additive three-lane architecture is still the right approach: keep trusted v1 safe, add richer declarative data, and isolate generated code in workers. The plan did miss two concrete correctness constraints that must be part of the architecture before execution: generated Remotion modules need an explicit persisted discriminant so they cannot fall through today's unknown-clip client route, and the compiled timeline contract needs a deliberate schema source of truth because Reigh local types and `@banodoco/timeline-schema` currently diverge. The revised document plan therefore adds these as foundation work, not optional implementation details.

The document at `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` should propose an additive architecture: preserve the current trusted sequence creator exactly as the default safe path, then add two higher-flexibility lanes behind explicit capabilities: a declarative schema-sequence lane and a generated Remotion module lane. The architecture must define contracts, validation, sandboxing, storage, migration, ArtAgents parity, testing, deployment, and rollout without implying raw untrusted code should be executed in the current editor/browser path.

## Phase 1: Document Current State And Invariants

### Step 1: Author the current-state map (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Document** the existing safe path in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: edge generation in `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.ts`, prompt/repair in `templates.ts`, duplicate frontend validator in `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/validation.ts`, trusted metadata in `metadata.ts`, draft insertion/replacement in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/sequence-drafts.ts`, and materialization in `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.ts`.
2. **Call out** the invariant that current trusted draft output is data-only: `{ clipType, hold, params }`, no JSX/code/imports/raw URLs/animation refs.
3. **Name** the existing renderer split in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts`: native clips use the existing client renderer; registered themed clips route to Banodoco/Remotion.
4. **Call out** the current routing hazard in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: `decideRenderRoute()` currently treats unknown clip types as client-renderable media fallback, so generated module routing cannot rely on a new unknown `clipType` alone.

### Step 2: Define non-negotiable constraints (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a constraints section to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` stating that the current trusted sequence path remains default, backwards-compatible, and deployable independently.
2. **Specify** that higher-flexibility generation must be capability-gated by lane, not by weakening the current validator.
3. **State** that persisted timelines continue to use `timelines.config` and `timelines.asset_registry`, with versioned saves through `update_timeline_versioned` or `timeline-import`.
4. **Require** generated-code timeline clips to carry an explicit artifact/lane discriminant, not just a novel clip type.
5. **Require** the cross-boundary timeline contract to be aligned before schema-sequence or ArtAgents import rollout.

## Phase 2: Canonical Contracts Before New Lanes

### Step 3: Resolve the timeline schema source of truth (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Document** the current schema mismatch in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: Reigh local `TimelineConfig` in `src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts` requires `output`, has optional `theme`, and allows `generation_defaults`; the shared `@banodoco/timeline-schema` package used by `supabase/functions/timeline-import/handler.ts` currently requires `theme`, makes `output` optional, and omits `generation_defaults`.
2. **Choose** `@banodoco/timeline-schema` as the cross-boundary source of truth after alignment, because `timeline-import`, ArtAgents parity, and Remotion worker contracts need one portable schema.
3. **Plan** the alignment explicitly in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: update the shared schema to represent the actual Reigh persisted shape, including `output`, optional/defaultable `theme`, and `generation_defaults` or a documented replacement field; then have Reigh local types and ArtAgents generated types derive from that aligned schema.
4. **State** that schema-sequence compilers must emit the aligned shared `TimelineConfig` plus `AssetRegistry`, not a Reigh-only shape that passes local TypeScript but fails `timeline-import`.
5. **Add** a compatibility rule: existing Reigh timelines without `theme` remain valid; import/export paths normalize missing theme to the default only where the renderer requires it.

### Step 4: Define the generated sequence artifact discriminant (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Document** the persisted discriminant in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` using existing clip allowance where possible: `clip.generation.sequence_lane` with values `trusted_sequence_v1`, `schema_sequence`, or `remotion_module`, plus `clip.generation.artifact_id` for artifact-backed lanes.
2. **State** that `remotion_module` clips must not depend on unknown `clipType` fallback routing. The clip may still have a display `clipType`, but the safety boundary is `generation.sequence_lane === 'remotion_module'`.
3. **Require** `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts` support: widen `RouterClipShape` to include the nested generation metadata, add a `RenderRouteDecision.reason` value such as `generated_remotion_module`, and branch in `decideRenderRoute()` before the unknown-clip client fallback.
4. **Require** `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.test.ts` coverage proving a `remotion_module` clip routes to Banodoco even when its `clipType` is unknown or absent.

## Phase 3: Target Architecture — Three Sequence Lanes

### Step 5: Design lane 1, trusted structured drafts (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Describe** the existing lane as `trusted_sequence_v1` in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`.
2. **Preserve** current clip types (`image-jump`, `section-hook`, `art-card`, `resource-card`, `cta-card`) and validation behavior.
3. **Document** its intended use: fast safe prompt-to-timeline drafting and editing with existing UI controls.
4. **State** that trusted v1 clips may optionally record `clip.generation.sequence_lane = 'trusted_sequence_v1'` later for observability, but v1 must not require artifact storage to keep working.

### Step 6: Design lane 2, declarative schema-sequences (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Add** a schema-sequence contract to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` for AI-authored animation data that is richer than v1 but still non-code: version, lane, duration, fps hints, tracks/clips, typed primitives, bindings, keyframes, transitions, asset refs, theme refs, constraints, and provenance.
2. **Anchor** it to the aligned shared timeline schema from Step 3 and existing Reigh serialization behavior in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.ts`, extending through explicit `params`, `generation`, and approved schema fields rather than arbitrary top-level keys.
3. **Define** a compiler step: `schema_sequence` -> aligned canonical `TimelineConfig` + `AssetRegistry`, then existing editor/render paths consume the result.
4. **Specify** validator behavior: JSON schema/Zod validation, finite numeric bounds, max clip/track/keyframe counts, only registry asset keys or signed/public storage keys, deterministic defaults, no code strings, no network URLs unless already present in the asset registry.
5. **Require** compiled schema-sequence clips to carry `clip.generation.sequence_lane = 'schema_sequence'` and `artifact_id` where an artifact row exists.

### Step 7: Design lane 3, generated Remotion modules (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Add** a generated-code lane to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` that treats AI-authored Remotion modules as isolated render artifacts, not inline editor runtime code.
2. **Define** module shape: manifest JSON, source bundle, entry component, input props schema, asset contract, duration/dimensions/fps contract, declared capabilities, dependency allowlist, and provenance.
3. **State** the execution boundary: generated modules render only in a worker sandbox/Remotion render service, never directly in the Reigh browser editor.
4. **Specify** sandboxing: no arbitrary npm install at render time, no Node filesystem/network except injected asset reads and output writes, static import allowlist, bundle-time lint/typecheck, timeout/memory/frame limits, deterministic props, console/log capture, and artifact quarantine on validation failure.
5. **Connect** to render routing through the explicit discriminant from Step 4: generated-code clips always route to Banodoco/Remotion when `clip.generation.sequence_lane === 'remotion_module'`, while editor preview shows a placeholder, last rendered preview, or schema-derived fallback.
6. **Require** negative routing tests in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.test.ts` so a generated module represented with an unknown `clipType` never falls into the client route.

## Phase 4: Reigh Data Model, Edge Functions, And Storage

### Step 8: Plan database and storage contracts (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Document** minimal additive tables/columns in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: a `generated_sequence_artifacts` table for manifests, schema payloads, code bundle refs, validation status, author/user/project/timeline ids, source model, hash, and timestamps.
2. **Avoid** replacing `timelines.config`; instead store compiled timeline clips in existing config and link clips to artifacts through `clip.generation.sequence_lane` and `clip.generation.artifact_id`, or through a narrow aligned-schema artifact reference if Step 3 extends the shared schema.
3. **Define** storage buckets/paths: keep media in `timeline-assets`; use a separate private bucket/path for generated module source/bundles/previews if code artifacts are introduced; final renders stay aligned with existing render output conventions.
4. **Preserve** RLS/user ownership semantics matching `timelines` and `timeline-assets`.
5. **Document** that generated module source storage is not a runtime dependency for browser preview; browser UI reads validation status and preview/render artifacts only.

### Step 9: Plan edge function changes (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Add** an edge-function section to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` describing an additive `ai-generate-sequence-v2` or versioned mode on `ai-generate-sequence`, with explicit `lane: trusted | schema | remotion_module`.
2. **Keep** `ai-generate-sequence` v1 responses compatible unless the caller opts into v2.
3. **Define** validation pipeline: prompt -> model output -> parse -> lane-specific validator -> repair loop -> artifact persistence -> compiled timeline preview payload.
4. **Document** imports/reuse from existing helpers: rate limit conventions, `bootstrapEdgeHandler`, `jsonResponse`, `toErrorMessage`, and structured invalid draft reporting.
5. **Specify** that v2 compiled outputs use the aligned shared schema from Step 3 and write the lane/artifact discriminant from Step 4.
6. **Specify** `timeline-import` integration for external/ArtAgents-written timeline updates, including UUID `project_id` and `timeline_id` requirements, project ownership checks, `expected_version` optimistic concurrency, force/current-version behavior, asset registry validation, and validation against the aligned shared schema.

### Step 10: Plan renderer contracts (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Document** renderer input/output contracts in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: canonical aligned `TimelineConfig`, `AssetRegistry`, theme id or defaulted theme, theme overrides, output filename, project/user/timeline ids, correlation id, lane metadata, and render artifact URL.
2. **Require** `materializeSequenceConfig`-style asset resolution before worker handoff for sequence params that expose component-facing URLs.
3. **Require** `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts` to inspect `generation.sequence_lane` before the existing unknown-clip fallback.
4. **Define** failure modes: validation failure, unsupported capability, module compile failure, render timeout, missing asset, schema mismatch, and version conflict should all be structured errors visible to the editor.

## Phase 5: Reigh Editor And Migration Path

### Step 11: Plan editor UX and migration (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** an editor integration section to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` that keeps `SequenceCreatorPanel` on trusted drafts by default.
2. **Define** opt-in controls for schema/code lanes: capability-gated generate mode, validation report, preview status, insert/replace behavior, and safe fallback when a generated module cannot be previewed interactively.
3. **Plan** migration from current trusted sequence creator: ship aligned schema and lane metadata first, then schema-sequence generation, then worker-only generated modules; no existing timeline rows require migration.
4. **State** that v1 trusted drafts can be compiled into the v2 artifact model later for observability, but this should be a passive migration, not a blocking launch task.
5. **Document** that generated module clips are previewed in the editor as status/thumbnail/proxy content, not by loading module source in the browser.

### Step 12: Plan ArtAgents parity (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Document** ArtAgents touch points in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: `artagents/timeline.py`, `artagents/render_remotion.py`, `artagents/open_in_reigh.py`, `artagents/publish.py`, `remotion/src/Root.tsx`, generated Remotion types, and `hype.timeline.json` / `hype.assets.json` outputs.
2. **Require** shared schema parity: ArtAgents should emit the same aligned declarative schema-sequence artifacts and compiled Reigh `TimelineConfig`/`AssetRegistry` as Reigh.
3. **Define** generated Remotion module parity: ArtAgents can author/render modules locally through its Remotion wrapper, then publish compatible artifacts to Reigh only after validation.
4. **Call out** the current limitation from `open_in_reigh.py`: local absolute asset paths must be uploaded or self-hosted before Reigh playback.
5. **Require** ArtAgents import/export examples to include UUID `project_id`/`timeline_id`, expected version handling, and artifact/lane metadata where generated sequence artifacts are present.

## Phase 6: Validation, Tests, Deployment, And Rollout

### Step 13: Plan automated validation and tests (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Add** a test matrix to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` with unit tests for validators, schema compilers, serialization, render routing, artifact persistence, edge handlers, and timeline import.
2. **Name** existing Reigh test anchors: `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.test.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/validation.test.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.test.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/sequence-drafts.test.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.test.ts`, `supabase/functions/timeline-import/handler.test.ts`, and timeline-agent harness tests under `supabase/functions/_tests/harness/`.
3. **Require** render-router tests that update `RouterClipShape`, `RenderRouteDecision.reason`, and `decideRenderRoute()` coverage for `generation.sequence_lane === 'remotion_module'`.
4. **Require** schema alignment tests proving in-editor saves, `timeline-import`, ArtAgents exports, and worker render payloads accept the same aligned `TimelineConfig` shape for `theme`, `output`, and `generation_defaults`.
5. **Name** ArtAgents test anchors: Remotion smoke tests, timeline schema contract tests, render wrapper tests, publish/open-in-Reigh tests, and generated registry/type generation checks.
6. **Require** negative tests for code injection, raw URLs, missing assets, oversized schemas, unsupported imports, generated modules with unknown clip types, timeouts, version conflicts, and cross-user access.

### Step 14: Plan deployment and rollout (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** a rollout section to `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`: feature flags by lane, internal allowlist, shadow validation, artifact-only dry runs, limited schema insertion, worker-only code render beta, then broader release.
2. **Define** observability: validation error counts, repair loop counts, compile/render failures, render duration, artifact hash, lane usage, schema mismatch failures, render route decisions, and user-visible failure rates.
3. **Include** rollback: disable schema/code lanes independently, leave trusted v1 operational, preserve existing timelines, and hide generated module insert controls without deleting artifacts.
4. **Require** rollback to leave the `renderRouter.ts` generated-module branch in place as a safety net even if module generation is disabled.

### Step 15: Final document verification (`docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` contains sections for declarative schema-sequence, generated Remotion modules, edge functions, renderer/data model contracts, validation/sandboxing, storage, ArtAgents parity, migration from trusted sequence creator, tests, deployment, and rollout.
2. **Verify** `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` explicitly states that the current safe trusted sequence path is preserved and remains the default.
3. **Verify** `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` explicitly addresses generated module routing via `generation.sequence_lane === 'remotion_module'`, `RouterClipShape`, `RenderRouteDecision.reason`, `decideRenderRoute()`, and `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.test.ts`.
4. **Verify** `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` explicitly addresses schema alignment across Reigh local types, `@banodoco/timeline-schema`, `timeline-import`, ArtAgents outputs, and worker render payloads.
5. **Verify** all concrete file references in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` match existing repository paths or are clearly labeled as proposed new files/tables/buckets.

## Execution Order
1. Write the current-state and invariants in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md` first so the plan is anchored in the existing system.
2. Document schema alignment and generated-module discriminants before describing new lanes; both are correctness gates for every flexible sequence path.
3. Define the three-lane architecture before database or edge changes; storage, renderer contracts, and editor behavior depend on the lane boundaries.
4. Document Reigh runtime contracts before ArtAgents parity so ArtAgents targets the same canonical shapes.
5. Finish with validation, deployment, and rollout because those sections depend on all earlier contracts.

## Validation Order
1. First verify the document path and section coverage in `docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md`.
2. Then check that current safe v1 behavior is explicitly preserved and not weakened.
3. Then check that generated Remotion modules have a concrete discriminant and render-router update/test requirements.
4. Then check that the timeline schema source of truth is chosen and alignment across Reigh, shared schema, `timeline-import`, ArtAgents, and workers is planned.
5. Finally check that tests and rollout include negative/security cases, ArtAgents parity, and rollback controls.

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the current imperative selection adapters in `useclipdrag.ts`, `usetimelinepersistence.ts`, `usepollsync.ts`, and `videoeditorprovider.tsx`. the mutable refs (`selectedclipidsref`, `additiveselectionref`, `selectedclipidref`, `selectedtrackidref`) are runtime adapters for drag/save/poll callers rather than durable domain state, so the plan's wording about putting 'refs/helpers' into the `timeline` slice is a design error unless it explicitly keeps store state separate from ref adapters rebuilt around that state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-166",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` and `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/tracklistrenderer.tsx`. `timelinecanvas` still computes shared `rowresizepreview`, `selectedtrackid`, and other drag-sensitive props centrally and passes them through every `sortablerow`, so moving context reads to narrow zustand selectors alone will not meet the gate that non-dragged rows stay at 0 rerenders during drag unless the plan also changes this row-prop fan-out.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-167",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the staged-vs-mounted add flow in `src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useaddtovideoeditor.ts`. that hook decides between immediate in-process drop and staged url/localstorage fallback by testing whether the safe timeline hooks return `null`, so a global `timelinestore.ts` with default state would change runtime behavior unless the migration preserves an explicit 'editor mounted' availability boundary.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-168",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.ts` and `src/tools/video-editor/contexts/videoeditorprovider.tsx` together. the current composition is synchronous and consumers like the pending-add drain and agentchat bridge read fresh `editor` values in the same render pass, so the plan's 'build values and write them into the zustand store' step is under-specified about whether hydration happens during render or in an effect, and either choice has real correctness risk if not designed carefully.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-183",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 7.4's dev-only warning guard against the repo's frontend environment conventions. the plan sketch uses `process.env.node_env !== 'production'`, but this vite react app uses `import.meta.env.dev` throughout `src/`; unless `process` is separately defined in the browser bundle, that selector path can throw a `referenceerror` when placeholders are emitted.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "selection-coordination-20260426-1613"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-184",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `useselectionstore` export against the intent-facade requirement. exporting the raw zustand selector hook makes every field in `selectionstorestate` selectable, including the low-level reducer functions that step 5 intends to remove from public hooks, so it technically reopens the public low-level mutation surface rather than making the intent commands the only reachable api.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "selection-coordination-20260426-1613"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-187",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 7.4's proposed `currentattachmentset.ts` import against `selectionstore.ts:690-699`. `useselectionstore` is currently a private non-exported function, while the plan creates a separate module that imports it from `./selectionstore`; followed literally, that new file will not compile unless the plan either exports a deliberately narrow selector api or colocates `usecurrentattachmentset()` inside `selectionstore.ts`.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the `usepinnedgroupsync()` call site in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479` against the proposed context-based interaction guard. because sync is called directly from `timelineeditor`, routing its guard through new editor-context plumbing is not the most direct fit for the actual caller and depends on extra provider/type changes that the step does not enumerate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "refactor-pinned-shot-groups-20260408-0508"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-138",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the caller contract of `useselectedmediaclips()` via [usetimelineeditordata](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). this hook's caller currently provides `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig`, not a resolved config object with `pinnedshotgroups`, so the plan still does not handle the actual arguments passed at that call site and needs to pull pinned groups from editor `data` instead.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-first-class-shot-20260409-2052"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual callers of the timeline-selection refs with `rg 'selectedclipidsref|additiveselectionref|selectedclipidref|selectedtrackidref' src/tools/video-editor`. save/poll paths consume `selectedclipidref` and `selectedtrackidref`, but `useclipdrag.ts`, `timelineeditor.tsx`, `videoeditorprovider.tsx`, and `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts` also consume `selectedclipidsref` and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan does not yet handle all caller shapes unless it adds a store-backed adapter for the multi-select refs as well.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `setselectedclipid(` callers in `useassetmanagement.ts`, `usecliptextoverlay.ts`, `propertiespanel.tsx`, and `previewpanel.tsx`. those callers pass concrete clip ids and `null` through the existing dispatch-like api, so any store action that replaces `setselectedclipid` must preserve the same semantics for clearing and reselection rather than narrowing the contract to a simpler single-purpose setter.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-175",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the real callers of the safe timeline hooks in `src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useaddtovideoeditor.ts`. that code passes through both mounted-editor values and the `null` outside-provider case, then uses `ops.registergenerationasset`, `ops.handleassetdrop`, and `data.resolvedconfig?.clips` when available, so any replacement api has to preserve both nullable access and the current immediate-drop contract rather than only exposing non-null store selectors.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` as a caller of `usetimelineeditordata()`. it only reads `dataref`, but it passes that mutable ref straight into `useclipresizegesture`, which relies on latest timeline data during pointer sequences; the plan does not yet say how this exact caller will receive a stable mutable adapter once the context is removed.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-177",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/videoeditorshell.tsx`, where `handlekeyboarddelete` combines `editordata.dataref.current` with `editordata.selectedclipids` to build a mutation before falling back to `handledeleteclips`. that caller needs synchronized latest-ref and latest-selection semantics, so splitting those reads across unrelated store selectors without an adapter can change behavior even if each field is individually correct.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-188",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual attachment merge callers: `agentchat.tsx` currently passes timeline clips from `useselectedmediaclips()` and gallery clips from `usegalleryselection().selectedgalleryclips` into the merge. because both caller paths supply `clipid`, a `clipid || url` key handles placeholder timeline clips but no longer matches the real gallery-vs-timeline same-url caller case that the old url-keyed merge handled.",

      "plan_ids": [

      ]

- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
